````math
$`\log(\left|\Pi\right|)`$ term. The recursive argument for subsequent
iterations ($`t>1`$) would then proceed from this new single-step bound,
with the complexity term propagating through the analysis of the policy
condition numbers $`\kappa_{t}`$.

### F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number $`\kappa_{1}`$

Our next objective is to establish a sharp upper bound for the coverage
parameter

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\kappa_{1}\ :=\ \mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\frac{1}{\pi_{\theta_{1}}\!\left(y_{1}^{*}(x)\mid x\right)}\right],\qquad y_{1}^{*}(x)\in\arg\max_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi_{\theta_{1}}(y\mid x).
``` |  | (121) |

Define the nonnegative random variable

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Z(x)\ :=\ \frac{1}{\pi_{\theta_{1}}\!\left(y_{1}^{*}(x)\mid x\right)}.
``` |  | (122) |

Its expectation admits the standard tail–integral representation:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}\big[Z(x)\big]\;=\;\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\big(Z(x)\geq t\big)\,\mathrm{d}t.
``` |  | (123) |

##### 1. Support of $`Z`$ and reduction of the integral.

By assumption there exists $`c\in(0,1]`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
c\ \leq\ \pi_{\theta_{1}}\!\left(y_{1}^{*}(x)\mid x\right)\ \leq\ 1,\qquad\forall\,x\in\mathcal{X}.
``` |  | (124) |

Consequently,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1\ \leq\ Z(x)\ \leq\ \frac{1}{c},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{P}\!\big(Z(x)\geq t\big)=\begin{cases}1,&t\in[0,1),\\[3.0pt]
0,&t>\frac{1}{c}.\end{cases}
``` |  | (125) |

Hence
Eq. [123](#A6.E123 "Equation 123 ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
reduces to

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}\bigl[Z(x)\bigr]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}1\,\mathrm{d}t\;+\;\int_{1}^{\frac{1}{c}}\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\bigl(Z(x)\geq t\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}t`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=1\;+\;\int_{1}^{\frac{1}{c}}\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\bigl(Z(x)\geq t\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}t.`$ |  | (126) |

##### 2. Relating the tail $`\mathbb{P}(Z\geq t)`$ to the round-1 failure bound.

For any $`t\geq 1`$, set $`1/t=1-\delta`$ so that
$`\delta=(t-1)/t\in(0,1]`$. Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[Z(x)\geq t\right]=\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\pi_{\theta_{1}}\bigl(y_{1}^{*}(x)\mid x\bigr)\leq\tfrac{1}{t}\right]=\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\pi_{\theta_{1}}\bigl(y_{1}^{*}(x)\mid x\bigr)\leq 1-\delta\right].`$ |  |

Invoking the round-1 bound (cf.
Eq. [120](#A6.E120 "Equation 120 ‣ F.4.2 Integration into the Global Proof Structure ‣ F.4 The Refined Statistical Error and Integration into the Main Proof ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"))
yields

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[Z(x)\geq t\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma\delta}\left(\kappa_{0}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}+\beta\log\kappa_{0}\right)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{t}{t-1}\,A,\qquad A\ :=\ \frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\kappa_{0}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}+\beta\log\kappa_{0}\right).`$ |  | (127) |

For notational convenience define

|     |                                        |     |       |
|-----|----------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       B(t)\ :=\ \frac{t}{t-1}\,A,\qquad t>1.
       ```                                     |     | (128) |

Since probabilities are at most $`1`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}\!\big(Z(x)\geq t\big)\ \leq\ \min\{1,\,B(t)\},\qquad t\in[1,1/c].
``` |  | (129) |

##### 3. Exact integral evaluation via the switch point $`t_{0}`$.

The function $`B(t)`$ is strictly decreasing on $`(1,\infty)`$, with
$`\lim_{t\downarrow 1}B(t)=+\infty`$ and
$`\lim_{t\uparrow\infty}B(t)=A`$. Hence there exists a unique
$`t_{0}\in(1,\infty)`$ solving $`B(t_{0})=1`$, namely

|     |                                                          |     |       |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       t_{0}\ =\ \frac{1}{1-A},\qquad\text{provided }A\in(0,1).
       ```                                                       |     | (130) |

We now distinguish two regimes:

*(i) Nontrivial regime: $`A<1`$ and $`t_{0}\leq 1/c`$.* Then

|     |                                                 |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \min\{1,B(t)\}=\begin{cases}1,&t\in[1,t_{0}],\\
       B(t),&t\in[t_{0},1/c],\end{cases}
       ```                                              |     |

and therefore

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\frac{1}{c}}\min\{1,B(t)\}\,\mathrm{d}t`$ | $`\displaystyle=(t_{0}-1)\;+\;\int_{t_{0}}^{1/c}B(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.`$ |  | (131) |

Using the antiderivative

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\int B(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\;=\;A\!\int\!\left(1+\frac{1}{t-1}\right)\mathrm{d}t\;=\;A\left(t+\ln|t-1|\right)+\mathrm{const},
``` |  | (132) |

we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{1/c}B(t)\,\mathrm{d}t`$ | $`\displaystyle=A\Bigl[\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{c}-t_{0}\Bigr)+\ln\!\Bigl(\tfrac{1/c-1}{t_{0}-1}\Bigr)\Bigr].`$ |  | (133) |

Combining
Eq. [126](#A6.E126 "Equation 126 ‣ 1. Support of 𝑍 and reduction of the integral. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
Eq. [131](#A6.E131 "Equation 131 ‣ 3. Exact integral evaluation via the switch point 𝑡_0. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
and
Eq. [133](#A6.E133 "Equation 133 ‣ 3. Exact integral evaluation via the switch point 𝑡_0. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
yields

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z(x)]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 1+(t_{0}-1)+A\Bigl[\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{c}-t_{0}\Bigr)+\ln\!\Bigl(\tfrac{1/c-1}{t_{0}-1}\Bigr)\Bigr].`$ |  | (134) |

*(ii) Saturated regime: either $`A\geq 1`$ or $`t_{0}>1/c`$.* In this
case $`\min\{1,B(t)\}\equiv 1`$ on $`[1,1/c]`$, hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[Z(x)]\ \leq\ 1+\left(\frac{1}{c}-1\right)\ =\ \frac{1}{c}.
``` |  | (135) |

##### 4. A concise, numerically stable upper bound.

To present a single bound covering both regimes, note that for
$`A\in(0,1)`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z(x)]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 1+(t_{0}-1)+A\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{c}-t_{0}\Bigr)+A\ln\!\Bigl(\tfrac{1/c-1}{t_{0}-1}\Bigr)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=t_{0}+A\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{c}-t_{0}\Bigr)+A\ln\!\Bigl(\tfrac{1/c-1}{t_{0}-1}\Bigr).`$ |  | (136) |

Using $`t_{0}=\frac{1}{1-A}`$ and the elementary bounds
$`\ln u\leq u-1`$ and $`\ln u\leq\ln(1/c)-\ln(t_{0}-1)`$ for
$`u=\frac{1/c-1}{t_{0}-1}`$, one arrives at the clean relaxation

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[Z(x)]\ \lesssim\ 1+\frac{1}{c}+\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{c}\Bigr)A,\qquad\text{whenever }A\in(0,1)\text{ and }t_{0}\leq\frac{1}{c}.
``` |  | (137) |

Combining
Eq. [137](#A6.E137 "Equation 137 ‣ 4. A concise, numerically stable upper bound. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
with the trivial bound
Eq. [135](#A6.E135 "Equation 135 ‣ 3. Exact integral evaluation via the switch point 𝑡_0. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
and recalling
Eq. [121](#A6.E121 "Equation 121 ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")–Eq. [122](#A6.E122 "Equation 122 ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
we get the final estimate

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\kappa_{1}\ =\ \mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\frac{1}{\pi_{\theta_{1}}(y_{1}^{*}(x)\mid x)}\right]\ \lesssim\ 1+\frac{1}{c}+\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{c}\Bigr)\,A\;\wedge\;\frac{1}{c}
``` |  | (138) |

(where $`a\wedge b:=\min\{a,b\}`$), with $`A`$ given by
Eq. [127](#A6.E127 "Equation 127 ‣ 2. Relating the tail ℙ(𝑍≥𝑡) to the round-1 failure bound. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")–Eq. [128](#A6.E128 "Equation 128 ‣ 2. Relating the tail ℙ(𝑍≥𝑡) to the round-1 failure bound. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").

##### 5. Specialization to the linear softmax model.

Substituting the explicit form of $`A`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle A\ =\ \frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\kappa_{0}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}+\beta\log\kappa_{0}\right),`$ |  |

into
Eq. [138](#A6.E138 "Equation 138 ‣ 4. A concise, numerically stable upper bound. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
and using the non-saturated branch yields the advertised bound

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\kappa_{1}\ \lesssim\ 1+\frac{1}{c}+\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{c}\Bigr)\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\kappa_{0}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}+\beta\log\kappa_{0}\right).
``` |  | (139) |

##### Remarks.

- (i)
  The constant $`A`$ aggregates the statistical error and regularization
  effects. The “nontrivial” case $`A<1`$ corresponds to the
  high–confidence regime where the failure tail is integrable with a
  logarithmic correction.
- (ii)
  The monotonicity of $`B(t)`$ justifies the unique switch point
  $`t_{0}`$ and the split–integral evaluation. The clean relaxation
  Eq. [137](#A6.E137 "Equation 137 ‣ 4. A concise, numerically stable upper bound. ‣ F.5 Bounding the Policy Condition Number 𝜅_1 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
  trades a small slack for algebraic transparency.
- (iii)
  The dependence on $`d`$ and $`n`$ enters through the
  covering–number–driven statistical term, producing the characteristic
  factor $`n^{-1/2}\!\bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\bigr)^{3/2}`$.

### F.6 Recursive Analysis and Asymptotic Behavior of Policy Condition Number

The analysis of the first iteration ($`t=1`$) provides the essential
foundation for a recursive framework that extends our performance
guarantees to an arbitrary number of iterations $`T`$. At its core, the
argument relies on the observation that the bound on the policy
performance at iteration $`t`$ is determined by two factors: (i) the
statistical accuracy of the update at that step, and (ii) the inherited
coverage coefficient from the previous iteration, $`\kappa_{t-1}`$. The
recursive nature of this dependency induces a dynamic system of bounds
whose long-term stability properties must be carefully analyzed.

##### Recursive Formulation.

By induction on $`t`$, the derivation for $`\kappa_{1}`$ extends
naturally to arbitrary $`t`$. Specifically, the coverage parameter at
iteration $`t`$ satisfies the recurrence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\kappa_{t}\ \lesssim\ 1+\frac{1}{c}+\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{c}\Bigr)\frac{1}{\gamma}\!\left(\kappa_{t-1}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}+\beta\log\kappa_{t-1}\right).
``` |  | (140) |

This recurrence encapsulates the interaction between statistical error,
represented by the square-root term, and regularization effects,
represented by the logarithmic term. The combination ensures that
although $`\kappa_{t}`$ may initially be large, successive iterations
prevent uncontrolled growth, enforcing stability in the long run.

##### Simplification via Constants.

To streamline the analysis, we define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{0}:=1+\frac{1}{c},\quad M_{1}:=\Bigl(1+\tfrac{1}{c}\Bigr)\frac{1}{\gamma\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\tfrac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2},\quad M_{2}:=\Bigl(1+\tfrac{1}{c}\Bigr)\frac{\beta}{\gamma}.
``` |  |

Substituting these definitions, the recurrence can be written more
compactly as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\kappa_{t}\ \leq\ M_{0}+M_{1}\sqrt{\kappa_{t-1}}+M_{2}\log\kappa_{t-1}.
``` |  | (141) |

The presence of both $`\sqrt{\kappa_{t-1}}`$ and $`\log\kappa_{t-1}`$
reflects the dual statistical–regularization structure of the update
process.

##### Bounding the Recurrence.

Although
Eq. [141](#A6.E141 "Equation 141 ‣ Simplification via Constants. ‣ F.6 Recursive Analysis and Asymptotic Behavior of Policy Condition Number ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
is nonlinear, we can simplify its analysis. Using the classical
inequality $`\log x\leq\tfrac{2}{e}\sqrt{x}`$ for $`x\geq 1`$ (a
condition satisfied since $`\kappa_{t-1}\geq 1`$ as an expectation of
inverse probabilities), we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\kappa_{t}\ \leq\ M_{0}+K\sqrt{\kappa_{t-1}},\qquad K:=M_{1}+\tfrac{2}{e}M_{2}.
``` |  | (142) |

This upper bound reduces the problem to a square-root recursion, which
admits tractable asymptotic analysis.

##### Fixed-Point Analysis.

The iterative map $`h(\kappa)=M_{0}+K\sqrt{\kappa}`$ has a unique
positive fixed point $`U`$, obtained by solving $`U=M_{0}+K\sqrt{U}`$.
Solving this quadratic in $`\sqrt{U}`$ yields

|     |                                                         |     |       |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       U\;=\;\left(\frac{K+\sqrt{K^{2}+4M_{0}}}{2}\right)^{2}.
       ```                                                      |     | (143) |

This fixed point represents the stable asymptotic value of the coverage
parameter. Its existence guarantees that the self-rewarding process
cannot diverge indefinitely; rather, it is inherently stable and bounded
by problem-specific constants.

##### Convergence Rate.

The contraction rate of the iteration is determined by the derivative of
$`h(\kappa)`$ at the fixed point:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h^{\prime}(\kappa)=\frac{K}{2\sqrt{\kappa}},\qquad h^{\prime}(U)=\frac{K}{2\sqrt{U}}.
``` |  |

Defining

|     |                                                               |     |       |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       q:=\frac{K}{2\sqrt{U}}=\frac{K}{K+\sqrt{K^{2}+4M_{0}}}\;<\;1,
       ```                                                            |     | (144) |

we observe that $`0<q<1`$, which implies that convergence to $`U`$ is
geometric. The deviation from the fixed point shrinks by a factor of at
least $`q`$ at each iteration, guaranteeing rapid stabilization.

##### Explicit Bound for Finite $`T`$.

This geometric contraction yields an explicit finite-$`T`$ bound:

|     |                                                 |     |       |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \kappa_{t}\;\leq\;U+q^{T}\big(\kappa_{0}-U\big)
       ```                                              |     | (145) |

for all $`T\geq 1`$. The residual dependence on the initialization
decays exponentially, while the asymptotic behavior is governed entirely
by $`U`$.

##### Asymptotic Behavior and Practical Implications.

To obtain further intuition, consider the regime where
$`\beta\lesssim n^{-1/2}`$, so that the statistical term $`M_{1}`$
dominates the regularization term $`M_{2}`$. In this case, the fixed
point satisfies

|     |                             |     |
|-----|-----------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       U\;\asymp\;M_{0}+M_{1}^{2},
       ```                          |     |

leading to the explicit bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\kappa_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim M_{0}+M_{1}^{2}+M_{2}^{2}\;+\;\Bigl(1+\sqrt{M_{0}/(M_{1}^{2}+M_{2}^{2})}\Bigr)^{-T}\kappa_{0}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\frac{1}{c}\;+\;\frac{1}{n}\Bigl(\frac{1}{c\gamma}\Bigr)^{2}\!\left(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\right)^{3}}_{\text{Asymptotic Stable Value}}\;+\;\underbrace{\bigl(1+\sqrt{nc}\bigr)^{-T}\kappa_{0}}_{\text{Decaying Initial Condition}}.`$ |  | (146) |

This final bound highlights two crucial features. First, the influence
of the initialization $`\kappa_{0}`$ vanishes exponentially fast with
$`T`$, ensuring robustness to poor starting conditions. Second, the
asymptotic stable value decreases as $`n`$ grows, confirming that larger
sample sizes yield improved coverage and reduced statistical
uncertainty. These insights reinforce the interpretation of the
self-rewarding process as not only stable but also statistically
efficient in the large-sample regime.

### F.7 Final Performance Bound for Arbitrary Iteration $`T`$

Having established the asymptotic stability of the coverage coefficient,
we are now prepared to present the main performance guarantee for the
policy $`\pi_{\theta_{T}}`$ after an arbitrary $`T`$ rounds of
self-rewarding training. The derivation closely parallels the
single-step analysis, but it crucially leverages our recursive
understanding of $`\kappa_{t}`$ across iterations.

##### From Coverage to Performance.

The probability that the policy $`\pi_{\theta_{T}}`$ assigns
insufficient probability mass to its own optimal response
$`y_{T}^{*}(x)`$ is controlled by its performance gap relative to the
KL-regularized optimal policy. This gap, in turn, depends on the
statistical error of the learning step at iteration $`T`$, which is
governed by the coverage coefficient of the previous iterate
$`\kappa_{t-1}`$. Formally, we obtain the $`T`$-step analogue of the
single-step bound:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\big[\pi_{\theta_{T}}(y_{T}^{*}(x)\mid x)\leq 1-\delta\big]`$ | $`\displaystyle\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{\gamma\delta}\!\left(\kappa_{t-1}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}\;+\;\beta\log\kappa_{t-1}\right).`$ |  | (147) |

This expression links the performance at iteration $`T`$ to the coverage
inherited from iteration $`T-1`$.

##### Substituting the Asymptotic Bound.

The crucial final step is to substitute into
Eq. [147](#A6.E147 "Equation 147 ‣ From Coverage to Performance. ‣ F.7 Final Performance Bound for Arbitrary Iteration 𝑇 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
our explicit asymptotic bound for $`\kappa_{t-1}`$ from
Eq. [146](#A6.E146 "Equation 146 ‣ Asymptotic Behavior and Practical Implications. ‣ F.6 Recursive Analysis and Asymptotic Behavior of Policy Condition Number ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").
This replacement eliminates the iteration-dependent and unknown
$`\kappa_{t-1}`$, yielding an expression in terms of fundamental
parameters of the problem $`(n,d,c)`$ and the initial condition
$`\kappa_{0}`$.

By taking the square root of the bound on $`\kappa_{t-1}`$ (and applying
the inequality $`\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}`$), we obtain a clean
decomposition. As $`T`$ grows, the dominant contribution arises from the
square root of the asymptotic stable value, which scales with $`1/c`$.
The residual dependence on the initialization decays exponentially and
quickly becomes negligible. Substituting these estimates back into
Eq. [147](#A6.E147 "Equation 147 ‣ From Coverage to Performance. ‣ F.7 Final Performance Bound for Arbitrary Iteration 𝑇 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
and noting that the $`\beta\log\kappa_{t-1}`$ term is of lower order, we
arrive at the final result:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\big[\pi_{\theta_{T}}(y_{T}^{*}(x)\mid x)\leq 1-\delta\big]`$ | $`\displaystyle\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{\gamma\delta\sqrt{n}}\Bigl(d\log\frac{nB}{d\rho}\Bigr)^{3/2}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{c}}+\bigl(1+\sqrt{nc}\bigr)^{-\frac{T-1}{2}}\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}\right).`$ |  | (148) |

##### Interpretation.

This inequality, the culmination of our analysis, provides several
insights into the behavior of the self-rewarding process for the linear
softmax model class:

- •
  Statistical Complexity. The leading error term scales as
  $`\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(d\log n)^{3/2}`$, quantifying the inherent
  learning difficulty of the parametric model. Performance improves as
  $`n`$ increases, while higher dimensionality $`d`$ incurs increased
  complexity. This replaces the inapplicable $`\log|\Pi|`$ term in the
  finite-class setting with a refined, distribution-dependent complexity
  measure.
- •
  Stability and Self-Correction. The second term inside the parentheses
  demonstrates exponential decay of the influence of the initial
  coverage $`\kappa_{0}`$. Even if the initial policy is poorly
  calibrated (large $`\kappa_{0}`$), its effect on performance vanishes
  rapidly as $`T`$ grows. This validates the intuition that the
  self-rewarding loop is self-correcting.
- •
  Long-Term Performance. In the limit $`T\to\infty`$, the decaying
  initialization term disappears entirely. The ultimate performance is
  dictated solely by the statistical error and the problem’s intrinsic
  difficulty, captured by $`c`$. This confirms that the process
  converges to a stable, initialization-independent error floor.

##### Conclusion.

Equation
[148](#A6.E148 "Equation 148 ‣ Substituting the Asymptotic Bound. ‣ F.7 Final Performance Bound for Arbitrary Iteration 𝑇 ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem : Performance Guarantees for Linear Softmax Models ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
completes the adaptation of the general recursive argument to the linear
softmax model class. It provides a rigorous and interpretable guarantee:
the model’s performance stabilizes to a predictable asymptotic rate,
while transient initialization effects are washed out exponentially
fast. This establishes both the robustness and the efficiency of
self-rewarding training in this setting.

## Appendix G Proof of Corollary [6.4](#S6.Thmtheorem4 "Corollary 6.4 (Linear-Softmax under Exponential Spectral Decay). ‣ 6 Application to Parametric Models: The Linear Softmax Case ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"): Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension

We strengthen the parametric guarantee by replacing the ambient
dimension $`d`$ in the statistical factor with a data-dependent
*effective dimension*. This mitigates “curse of dimensionality” and
better reflects the spectral structure of the feature covariance under
the data distribution.

###### Definition G.1 (Ridge effective dimension).

For any $`\lambda>0`$ define the ridge effective dimension

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\;:=\;\mathrm{Tr}\!\left(\Sigma_{\varphi}\big(\Sigma_{\varphi}+\lambda I\big)^{-1}\right)\;=\;\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i}+\lambda}\,,
``` |  |

where $`\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}`$ are the eigenvalues of
$`\Sigma_{\varphi}`$ in nonincreasing order. It holds that
$`0\leq d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\leq\mathrm{rank}(\Sigma_{\varphi})\leq d`$
and $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)`$ is nonincreasing in $`\lambda`$. In
the isotropic case $`\Sigma_{\varphi}=I`$ and for $`\lambda\ll 1`$ we
have $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\approx d`$.

###### Lemma G.2 (Metric entropy with covariance geometry).

Let
$`\mathcal{F}:=\{f_{\theta}(x.y)=\langle\theta,\varphi(x,y)\rangle:\|\theta\|_{2}\leq B\}`$.
For the $`L_{2}(\mu)`$ metric induced by $`\Sigma_{\varphi}`$, the
$`\varepsilon`$-covering number of $`\mathcal{F}`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log\mathcal{N}\!\big(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mu)\big)\;\lesssim\;d_{\mathrm{eff}}\!\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{B^{2}}\Big)\cdot\log\!\Bigg(\frac{c\,B}{\varepsilon}\Bigg)\,,
``` |  |

for a universal constant $`c>0`$. Consequently, whenever an argument in
the proof of
Theorem [6.2](#S6.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 6.2 (Performance Guarantee for Linear Softmax Models). ‣ 6 Application to Parametric Models: The Linear Softmax Case ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
uses the ambient-dimension entropy bound
$`\log\mathcal{N}\lesssim d\log(c^{\prime}B/\varepsilon)`$, it can be
replaced by the covariance-adaptive bound above.

*Proof of Lemma
[G.2](#A7.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma G.2 (Metric entropy with covariance geometry). ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").*
Diagonalize $`\Sigma_{\varphi}=U\Lambda U^{\top}`$ and reparameterize
$`\theta=Uz`$. The $`L_{2}(\mu)`$-norm is
$`\|f_{\theta}\|_{L_{2}(\mu)}^{2}=\theta^{\top}\Sigma_{\varphi}\theta=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}z_{i}^{2}`$.
Cover the ellipsoid $`\{z:\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}z_{i}^{2}\leq B^{2}\}`$ by
axis-aligned slabs with granularity matched to $`\lambda_{i}^{1/2}`$.
Summing the logarithms of the per-axis covering counts yields
$`\sum_{i}\log\big(1+cB\lambda_{i}^{1/2}/\varepsilon\big)`$. A monotone
integral comparison together with the ridge truncation
$`\lambda_{i}/(\lambda_{i}+\varepsilon^{2}/B^{2})`$ gives the stated
$`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\varepsilon^{2}/B^{2})`$ dependence. $`\square`$

##### Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension.

The following elementary bounds will be useful:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i}+\lambda}\;\leq\;\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}\{\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda\}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i:\,\lambda_{i}<\lambda}\lambda_{i}\;\leq\;\operatorname{rank}(\Sigma_{\varphi})\;\wedge\;\frac{\operatorname{Tr}(\Sigma_{\varphi})}{\lambda}.
``` |  | (149) |

We quantify $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)`$ under concrete spectral
assumptions and then substitute it into the parametric bound of
Theorem [6.2](#S6.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 6.2 (Performance Guarantee for Linear Softmax Models). ‣ 6 Application to Parametric Models: The Linear Softmax Case ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").

###### Assumption G.3 (Spectral regimes).

Let $`\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}`$ be the spectrum of
$`\Sigma_{\varphi}`$ in nonincreasing order. We consider the following
cases.

1.  (A)
    Exponential decay. There exist constants $`C>0`$ and $`\alpha>0`$
    such that $`\lambda_{i}\leq C\,\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha i}`$ for all
    $`i`$.
2.  (B)
    Polynomial decay. There exist constants $`C>0`$ and $`p>1`$ such
    that $`\lambda_{i}\leq C\,i^{-p}`$ for all $`i`$.
3.  (C)
    Spiked$`+`$small tail. There is an integer $`r\geq 1`$ and
    $`\vartheta>0`$ such that $`\lambda_{i}\geq\vartheta`$ for
    $`i\leq r`$ and $`\sum_{i>r}\lambda_{i}=\tau`$ with $`\tau>0`$
    possibly small.

###### Lemma G.4 (Effective dimension under Assumption [G.3](#A7.Thmtheorem3 "Assumption G.3 (Spectral regimes). ‣ Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension. ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")).

Under
Assumption [G.3](#A7.Thmtheorem3 "Assumption G.3 (Spectral regimes). ‣ Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension. ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
the following bounds hold for all $`\lambda>0`$.

1.  (A)
    *Exponential decay implies logarithmic scaling:*

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\;\lesssim\;\log\!\Big(\frac{C}{\lambda}\Big)\,.
    ``` |  |

    In particular, choosing $`\lambda\asymp c/n`$ gives
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\lesssim\log n`$.
2.  (B)
    *Polynomial decay yields a power-law in $`\lambda`$:*

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\;\lesssim\;\Big(\frac{C}{\lambda}\Big)^{\!1/p}\,.
    ``` |  |

    In particular, choosing $`\lambda\asymp c/n`$ gives
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\lesssim n^{1/p}`$.
3.  (C)
    *Spiked$`+`$small tail gives $`r+\mathcal{O}(1)`$ when $`\lambda`$
    dominates the tail:*

    |     |                                                                |     |
    |-----|----------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
    |     |
           ``` math
           d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\;\leq\;r\;+\;\frac{\tau}{\lambda}\,.
           ```                                                             |     |

    Hence for any $`\lambda\geq\tau`$ we have
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\leq r+1`$. If moreover
    $`r=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$, then
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$.

*Proof of Lemma
[G.4](#A7.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma G.4 (Effective dimension under Assumption ). ‣ Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension. ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").*
(A) Let $`m=\big\lceil\alpha^{-1}\log(C/\lambda)\big\rceil`$. Then
$`\lambda_{m}\lesssim\lambda`$ and thus
$`\#\{i:\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda\}\leq m\lesssim\log(C/\lambda)`$. The
tail sum obeys
$`\sum_{i>m}\lambda_{i}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha m}\lesssim\lambda`$,
so the second term in
Eq. [149](#A7.E149 "Equation 149 ‣ Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension. ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
is $`\mathcal{O}(1)`$. (B) Let
$`m=\big\lceil(C/\lambda)^{1/p}\big\rceil`$ so that
$`\lambda_{m}\lesssim\lambda`$. Then
$`\#\{\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda\}\leq m\lesssim(C/\lambda)^{1/p}`$ and
$`\sum_{i>m}\lambda_{i}\lesssim\int_{m}^{\infty}x^{-p}\mathrm{d}x\asymp m^{1-p}`$,
making the tail contribution
$`\lesssim m^{1-p}/\lambda\asymp(C/\lambda)^{1/p}`$. (C) Split the sum
at $`r`$ and use
Eq. [149](#A7.E149 "Equation 149 ‣ Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension. ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
on the tail:
$`\sum_{i>r}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i}+\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i>r}\lambda_{i}=\tau/\lambda`$.
$`\square`$

###### Corollary G.5 (Parametric bound with effective dimension).

Under conditions of
Theorem [6.2](#S6.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 6.2 (Performance Guarantee for Linear Softmax Models). ‣ 6 Application to Parametric Models: The Linear Softmax Case ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
fix any $`\lambda>0`$. With probability at least $`1-\rho`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\pi_{\theta_{T}}\!\big(y_{T}^{\ast}(x)\mid x\big)\leq 1-\delta\right]\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\gamma\,\delta}\,\Bigg(\,d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\cdot\log\!\frac{nB}{\rho}\,\Bigg)^{\!3/2}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{c}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}\,\big(1+\sqrt{nc}\big)^{-(T-1)/2}\Bigg)\,.
``` |  |

*Proof* Repeat the proof of
Theorem [6.2](#S6.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 6.2 (Performance Guarantee for Linear Softmax Models). ‣ 6 Application to Parametric Models: The Linear Softmax Case ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
and
Corollary [5.7](#S5.Thmtheorem7 "Corollary 5.7 (Iterations to Suppress Initialization Effects). ‣ 5 Finite-Sample Analysis for Iterative Self-Rewarding Alignment ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models")
replacing the ambient-dimension entropy bound by
Lemma [G.2](#A7.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma G.2 (Metric entropy with covariance geometry). ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").
The empirical Bernstein step and the iterative self-correction argument
are unchanged, yielding the same stability–transient factor
$`\big(1/\sqrt{c}+\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}(1+\sqrt{nc})^{-(T-1)/2}\big)`$ and
the covering-number contribution with $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)`$.
$`\square`$

###### Corollary G.6 (Substitution into the parametric risk bound).

Under the conditions of
Theorem [6.2](#S6.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 6.2 (Performance Guarantee for Linear Softmax Models). ‣ 6 Application to Parametric Models: The Linear Softmax Case ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models"),
the following explicit forms hold when $`d`$ in the statistical factor
is replaced by $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)`$.

1.  (A)
    If $`\Sigma_{\varphi}`$ has exponential spectral decay and
    $`\lambda\asymp c/n`$, then

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\pi_{\theta_{T}}(y_{T}^{\ast}(x)\mid x)\leq 1-\delta\right]\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\gamma\delta}\,\Big(\log n\cdot\log\frac{nB}{\rho}\Big)^{3/2}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{c}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}(1+\sqrt{nc})^{-(T-1)/2}\Big).
    ``` |  |

    Thus the dependence on $`d`$ is replaced by a *double logarithm in
    $`n`$*.
2.  (B)
    If $`\Sigma_{\varphi}`$ has polynomial decay of order $`p>1`$ and
    $`\lambda\simeq c/n`$, then

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\pi_{\theta_{T}}(y_{T}^{\ast}(x)\mid x)\leq 1-\delta\right]\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\gamma\delta}\,\Big(n^{1/p}\cdot\log\frac{nB}{\rho}\Big)^{3/2}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{c}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}(1+\sqrt{nc})^{-(T-1)/2}\Big).
    ``` |  |

    Here the ambient dimension $`d`$ no longer appears. The rate is
    governed by the spectral exponent $`p`$.
3.  (C)
    In the spiked$`+`$small-tail model with rank $`r`$ spike and tail
    mass $`\tau`$, any $`\lambda\geq\tau`$ gives

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}\!\left[\pi_{\theta_{T}}(y_{T}^{\ast}(x)\mid x)\leq 1-\delta\right]\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\gamma\delta}\,\Big((r+1)\cdot\log\frac{nB}{\rho}\Big)^{3/2}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{c}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}(1+\sqrt{nc})^{-(T-1)/2}\Big).
    ``` |  |

    If $`r=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$, the $`d`$-dependence is reduced to
    $`\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$ inside the statistical factor.

###### Remark G.7 (Choice and interpretation of $`\lambda`$).

The parameter $`\lambda`$ is an *analysis device* that interpolates
between ambient and intrinsic complexity.

- •
  In isotropic or full-rank regimes with flat spectra, taking
  $`\lambda\downarrow 0`$ recovers
  $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\approx d`$.
- •
  With spectral decay, e.g., $`\lambda_{i}\asymp i^{-p}`$ for $`p>1`$,
  one has $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\ll d`$ for moderate $`\lambda`$,
  tightening the bound.
- •
  A practical choice is $`\lambda\asymp c\big/\!n`$ (or any monotone
  schedule $`\lambda_{T}`$), which trades a slightly larger bias in the
  entropy radius for a sharply reduced $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)`$.
  The contraction threshold in $`T`$ remains unaffected because it
  depends only on $`n`$ and $`c`$.

###### Remark G.8 (When does $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)`$ look like $`\log d`$?).

Two representative scenarios lead to a logarithmic-in-$`d`$ dependence.

1.  (i)
    *Log-rank energy concentration.* Suppose the spectrum concentrates
    on $`r=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$ leading directions, in the sense that
    $`\sum_{i>r}\lambda_{i}\leq\tau`$ with $`\tau`$ small. Taking
    $`\lambda\geq\tau`$ gives
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\leq r+1=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$ by
    Lemma [G.4](#A7.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma G.4 (Effective dimension under Assumption ). ‣ Explicit regimes for the ridge effective dimension. ‣ Appendix G Proof of Corollary : Data-Dependent Bounds via Effective Dimension ‣ Why Self-Rewarding Works: Theoretical Guarantees forIterative Alignment of Language Models").
2.  (ii)
    *Geometric decay until the ambient cutoff.* If
    $`\lambda_{i}\asymp C\,\rho^{\,i}`$ with $`\rho\in(0.1)`$, then for
    any $`\lambda`$ above the tail floor one has
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\asymp\log(C/\lambda)`$. If additionally
    $`\lambda`$ is chosen proportional to
    $`\tau:=\sum_{i>\tilde{r}}\lambda_{i}`$ for some
    $`\tilde{r}=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$, then
    $`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)\lesssim\tilde{r}+\mathcal{O}(1)=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$.

In both cases, substituting
$`d_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda)=\mathcal{O}(\log d)`$ into the statistical
factor replaces $`\big(d\log(\frac{nB}{d\rho})\big)^{3/2}`$ by
$`\big(\log d\cdot\log(\frac{nB}{\rho})\big)^{3/2}`$.
````
